To solve this problem, let's follow the steps outlined in the evaluation process:

1. **Examine the two black nodes:** The black nodes are labeled G and I.

2. **Examine the immediate neighboring nodes of G:**
   - G is connected to H, E, and B.

3. **Examine the immediate neighboring nodes of I:**
   - I is connected to H, P, and C.

4. **Iteratively find other neighboring nodes:**
   - From H, we find connections to F, K, X.
   - From E, we find connections to L, M, F, X.
   - From B, we find connections to N, Y, C.
   - From P, we find connections to J, K, O, D.
   - From C, we find connections to O, Y.

5. **Evaluate the found paths:**
   - Paths between G and I:
     - G-H-I
     - G-H-F-X-E-M-K-F-X-X-I
     - G-H-F-X-E-L
     - G-E-F-X-X-I
     - G-E-F-X-K-J-P-I
     - G-H-K-J-P-I
     - G-H-K-X-E-M-F
     - G-B-C-O-P-I
     - G-B-N-Y-C-O-P-I
   - To disconnect G and I, we need to remove edges such that all these paths are broken.

6. **Check if the removal of one edge suffices:**
   - Removing any single edge will not disconnect G and I because there are multiple paths.

7. **Compare if the removal of edges results in the smallest set of edges to be removed:**
   - Removing edges (G, H), (H, I), and (B, C) will disconnect G and I.
   - Check if fewer edges can be removed to achieve the same result.

After evaluating the paths and potential cuts, the minimal set of edges to remove to ensure G and I are completely disconnected is:

Edges to remove: 3, Cut Set: [(G, H), (H, I), (B, C)].

   - The black nodes are E and H.

2. **Find all immediate neighbors of the black nodes:**
   - Neighbors of E: F, H, M, L
   - Neighbors of H: E, B, I, C

3. **Trace all paths between E and H:**
   - Path 1: E → H
   - Path 2: E → F → G → X → K → F (cycle through F)
   - Path 3: E → F → B → H
   - Path 4: E → F → I → H
   - Path 5: E → F → I → C → H
   - Path 6: E → M → L → J → P → I → H
   - Path 7: E → M → L → J → P → O → N → C → H

4. **Identify the bottleneck nodes:**
   - To disconnect E and H, we need to cut all paths between them.
   - By removing node F, we cut paths E → F → B → H, E → F → I → H, and E → F → I → C → H.
   - By removing node I, we cut paths E → M → L → J → P → I → H and E → F → I → H.
   - By removing node C, we cut path E → F → I → C → H and E → M → L → J → P → O → N → C → H.

   Removing nodes F, I, and C will disconnect E and H.

5. **Verify the answer:**
   - Removing nodes F, I, and C will ensure that there are no paths left between E and H.

2. **Node H is connected to**: B, G, I, C.

- B -> C -> H
- B -> G -> H
- B -> N -> (intermediate nodes) ... -> H
- B -> G -> E -> L -> ... -> H
- B -> G -> X -> F -> M -> J -> P -> I -> H

Since there are multiple paths between B and H, we need to identify the critical edges that, when removed, will ensure no paths remain between B and H.

To minimize the number of edges to remove, we should focus on removing edges that are common across multiple paths:

1. **Remove the edge (B, G):**
   - This removes the direct paths: B -> G -> H and B -> G -> X -> F -> M -> J -> P -> I -> H.

2. **Remove the edge (B, C):**
   - This removes the path: B -> C -> H.

3. **Remove the edge (G, H):**
   - This removes the remaining path: B -> N -> (intermediate nodes) ... -> G -> H.

By removing these three edges, all paths between B and H are disconnected.

To solve the problem of disconnecting the two black nodes (N and G) in the given graph, we need to identify the minimum number of nodes that must be removed to ensure there are no paths between N and G.

Let's analyze the paths step-by-step:

1. **Identify direct neighbors of N and G:**
   - N is connected to: C, B, O
   - G is connected to: F, K, H

2. **Expand the search to second-level neighbors:**
   - Neighbors of N's neighbors:
     - C: connects to N, B, H, I, P
     - B: connects to N, C, E, O
     - O: connects to N, B, Y
   - Neighbors of G's neighbors:
     - F: connects to G, K, X, C
     - K: connects to G, F, X
     - H: connects to C, G, I, E, P

3. **Identify common neighbors and potential cut nodes:**
   - Common neighbors of N and G: None directly
   - Indirect common neighbors (via paths):
     - C (connected to N and G via different paths)
     - P (connected to N via C and to G via H)
     - I (connected to N via C and to G via H)
     - E (connected to N via B and to G via H)

4. **Determine the minimal cut set:**
   - Removing C, P, and E will disconnect all paths between N and G:
     - Removing C cuts the path N-C-H-G
     - Removing P cuts the path N-C-P-H-G
     - Removing E cuts the path N-B-E-H-G

Thus, the minimum number of nodes that must be removed to ensure N and G are completely disconnected is 3.

```plaintext
Nodes to remove: 3, Cut Set: [C, P, E]
```

   - D is connected to P.

   - Node F is connected to X and K.
   - Node B is connected to C and N.
   - Node P is connected to I and J.

   - Node X is connected to K and G.
   - Node C is connected to B, N, and O.
   - Node I is connected to H, G, and P.
   - Node K is connected to X and G.
   - Node G is connected to X, H, and I.
   - Node H is connected to G and I.

   - Path 1: E -> F -> X -> G -> H -> I -> P -> D

   - Path 3: E -> B -> C -> O -> P -> D
   - Path 4: E -> B -> C -> N -> B -> C -> O -> P -> D
   - Path 5: E -> B -> C -> N -> I -> P -> D

- Removing the edge (C, O) will disconnect Path 3 and Path 4.

   - Path 2: F -> E -> L -> M -> I -> J

   - Path 4: F -> G -> H -> B -> C -> N -> O -> I -> J
